{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_ad598d859b224ae58b3c13ca","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Validate tool input against the registered inputSchema\n2\t\n3\tIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#37\n4\tLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:M, area:tools, epic:extension-points\n5\t\n6\tPart of #35\n7\t\n8\t## Why\n9\t\n10\t`ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\n11\twithout ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\n12\tEvery tool declares a JSON Schema (`src/types/ai.ts`) that is handed verbatim to both\n13\tproviders, but nothing validates what comes back from the model. The shipped example shows the\n14\tshape of the problem: `example/weather-tool.ts` declares `location` as a required string, then\n15\tcasts and defaults it (`(input.location as string) || 'Unknown'`) with a comment warning the reader\n16\tthat `inputSchema` shapes what the model sends but does not enforce it. That is the careful version,\n17\twritten by the people who wrote the engine — and it still answers a malformed call with a\n18\tconfidently formatted forecast for `'Unknown'` rather than telling the model it got the call wrong.\n19\t\n20\tThe registry currently pushes this check onto each tool's own executor, leaving every consumer\n21\tto implement it itself — differently each time, or not at all. A tool that receives unexpected input must fail\n22\tin one predictable, known way before the rest of this epic's tool work can be built on top\n23\tof it.\n24\t\n25\t## What to do\n26\t\n27\tValidate `input` against the resolved tool definition's `inputSchema` inside\n28\t`ToolRegistry.execute`, before the executor runs, using a JSON Schema validator (`ajv`\n29\tunless the build proves otherwise) added to `dependencies` — not a second, hand-written\n30\tschema in a different format, since the schema already has to match what is handed to the\n31\tproviders.\n32\t\n33\tCompile a static tool's schema once at `register` time so a broken schema fails at startup,\n34\tnot mid-conversation; a schema produced by a function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource`\n35\tresolves and compiles per call, cached by the resolved schema object.\n36\t\n37\tA rejection returns a `ToolResponse` (`{result: '<what was wrong>'}`) rather than throwing,\n38\tso a malformed call becomes something the model can read and correct within the existing\n39\t`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5` budget, instead of ending the whole conversation with a\n40\tgeneric transport error. Validation enforces `required`, declared types and `enum` only —\n41\tit does not inject `additionalProperties: false`, coerce types, or apply defaults, and\n42\tthere is no opt-out flag.\n43\t\n44\tUpdate `docs/adding-a-tool.md`, `README.md` and `.specify/spec.md` to match.\n45\t\n46\t## Acceptance criteria\n47\t\n48\t- [ ] `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved definition's\n49\t      `inputSchema` before calling the executor; on failure it returns a `ToolResponse`\n50\t      whose `result` describes the failure, and the executor is never invoked\n51\t- [ ] A new `src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts` (the registry has no test file today)\n52\t      registers a tool requiring a `location` string, calls `execute` with `{}`, and\n53\t      asserts the returned result names `location` as required and the executor's call\n54\t      count is `0`\n55\t- [ ] A test asserts valid input with an unlisted extra property is passed through to the\n56\t      executor unchanged (`toEqual`, so an added default or coerced value fails the test) —\n57\t      exactly once\n58\t- [ ] A test asserts a wrong-typed field (a number where a string is required) is rejected\n59\t      with a message naming the field path and the expected type, and that the message does\n60\t      **not** contain the offending value\n61\t- [ ] A test asserts `register` throws at registration time for an uncompilable schema, and\n62\t      that the thrown message names the tool\n63\t- [ ] A test asserts a function-valued tool definition whose returned schema differs between\n64\t      calls is validated against the schema current at that call, not a stale cached one\n65\t- [ ] A test asserts a static tool's schema is compiled once across ten `execute` calls\n66\t- [ ] A tool-loop test in `chatOrchestrator.test.ts` drives a stub provider through a\n67\t      rejected tool call followed by a corrected retry, and asserts `processMessage`\n68\t      resolves — not rejects — with the rejection text visible to the provider on the next\n69\t      round\n70\t- [ ] `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name, unchanged,\n71\t      pinned by a test\n72\t- [ ] Rejection messages and log lines name the failing property paths and the constraint\n73\t      that failed, and never a property **value**. Tool input is where identifying data\n74\t      enters the engine and the engine redacts nothing today. A test asserts a rejected\n75\t      payload containing an identifying field produces no log line and no returned text\n76\t      containing that field's value\n77\t- [ ] `execute` carries a **one-line** doc comment — `re-lint/max-comment-lines` rejects a\n78\t      `@param`/`@returns`/`@throws` block — and the full contract (rejection `ToolResponse` for input\n79\t      the schema rejects, throw only for an unknown tool name) is stated in\n80\t      `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`, which the comment points at\n81\t- [ ] The validator is in `package.json` `dependencies`, not `peerDependencies` or\n82\t      `devDependencies`, and\n83\t      `npm run build && node --input-type=module -e \"import('./dist/index.js')\"` exits `0`\n84\t- [ ] `README.md` states what the registry now does, and the claim is true rather than\n85\t      merely present\n86\t- [ ] `docs/adding-a-tool.md` no longer tells executors to cast defensively and handle\n87\t      missing input; it states that input reaching an executor has already passed the\n88\t      declared schema, that unknown keys may still be present, and that a rejection is\n89\t      returned to the model rather than thrown\n90\t- [ ] `.specify/spec.md` records that a rejection is a tool result (not an exception), that\n91\t      the tool-round bound is what stops a retry cycle from spinning, and that extra\n92\t      properties pass through unmodified\n93\t- [ ] The PR body states the migration note: an executor that used to hand-validate its\n94\t      input now receives only input the schema already accepts, and a tool that relied on\n95\t      seeing malformed input to produce its own error message stops seeing it. The version\n96\t      bump is MINOR\n97\t- [ ] Every test this issue adds cites the statement it validates with an inline\n98\t      `([validated by ...])` link in a `specs/**` or `adrs/**` file, and the statements exist\n99\t      before the tests do. `re-lint/require-spec-link` fails the build on an uncited test, so the\n100\t      spec edits are part of this issue rather than a follow-up\n101\t- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry for this change, written for somebody reading it on npm\n102\t      rather than somebody reading this repo's log. CI runs `scripts/check-changelog.sh` and\n103\t      fails any PR touching a non-test file under `src/` without one\n104\t- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`)\n105\t      and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the changed public surface\n106\t\n107\t## Tech notes\n108\t\n109\tToday's `throw` in `registry.ts` propagates through the orchestrator's tool-call\n110\t`Promise.all`, out of the processing loop's `try`, fires `onError`, and reaches the\n111\tWebSocket error handler, which sends a generic \"Failed to process message\" and never sends\n112\tthe stream-end frame. That end-to-end path is why a rejection has to be a returned\n113\t`ToolResponse` instead of a throw.\n114\t\n115\tDeliberately out of scope: the `Unknown tool: <name>` throw stays as-is — the same failure\n116\tclass handled the opposite way, which is an intentional split, but changing it affects\n117\tdirect callers and is tracked as *Return an unknown tool name to the model instead of\n118\tthrowing*. Also out of scope: validating tool *output*; hallucination detection and an\n119\tevaluation harness; per-tool authorization — a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still\n120\tvalid, and authorization is a separate `ToolContext` boundary concern that no issue in this epic\n121\towns (*Let a consumer decline a tool call through a `beforeToolCall` hook* is a veto seam, not an\n122\tauthorization model); retry, timeout\n123\tand rate limiting around tool execution; and any actual tool implementation, which is\n124\twritten against this contract later.\n125\t\n126\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6a065b92-69cb-4751-8403-4aa4f3bdee06","uuid":"ac1c50a0-3e34-4c79-8db9-342dfb0d19dc","timestamp":"2026-11-02T23:03:07.444Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/b3d496cb9011/workspace/ticket.md","content":"# Validate tool input against the registered inputSchema\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#37\nLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:M, area:tools, epic:extension-points\n\nPart of #35\n\n## Why\n\n`ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\nwithout ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\nEvery tool declares a JSON Schema (`src/types/ai.ts`) that is handed verbatim to both\nproviders, but nothing validates what comes back from the model. The shipped example shows the\nshape of the problem: `example/weather-tool.ts` declares `location` as a required string, then\ncasts and defaults it (`(input.location as string) || 'Unknown'`) with a comment warning the reader\nthat `inputSchema` shapes what the model sends but does not enforce it. That is the careful version,\nwritten by the people who wrote the engine — and it still answers a malformed call with a\nconfidently formatted forecast for `'Unknown'` rather than telling the model it got the call wrong.\n\nThe registry currently pushes this check onto each tool's own executor, leaving every consumer\nto implement it itself — differently each time, or not at all. A tool that receives unexpected input must fail\nin one predictable, known way before the rest of this epic's tool work can be built on top\nof it.\n\n## What to do\n\nValidate `input` against the resolved tool definition's `inputSchema` inside\n`ToolRegistry.execute`, before the executor runs, using a JSON Schema validator (`ajv`\nunless the build proves otherwise) added to `dependencies` — not a second, hand-written\nschema in a different format, since the schema already has to match what is handed to the\nproviders.\n\nCompile a static tool's schema once at `register` time so a broken schema fails at startup,\nnot mid-conversation; a schema produced by a function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource`\nresolves and compiles per call, cached by the resolved schema object.\n\nA rejection returns a `ToolResponse` (`{result: '<what was wrong>'}`) rather than throwing,\nso a malformed call becomes something the model can read and correct within the existing\n`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5` budget, instead of ending the whole conversation with a\ngeneric transport error. Validation enforces `required`, declared types and `enum` only —\nit does not inject `additionalProperties: false`, coerce types, or apply defaults, and\nthere is no opt-out flag.\n\nUpdate `docs/adding-a-tool.md`, `README.md` and `.specify/spec.md` to match.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved definition's\n      `inputSchema` before calling the executor; on failure it returns a `ToolResponse`\n      whose `result` describes the failure, and the executor is never invoked\n- [ ] A new `src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts` (the registry has no test file today)\n      registers a tool requiring a `location` string, calls `execute` with `{}`, and\n      asserts the returned result names `location` as required and the executor's call\n      count is `0`\n- [ ] A test asserts valid input with an unlisted extra property is passed through to the\n      executor unchanged (`toEqual`, so an added default or coerced value fails the test) —\n      exactly once\n- [ ] A test asserts a wrong-typed field (a number where a string is required) is rejected\n      with a message naming the field path and the expected type, and that the message does\n      **not** contain the offending value\n- [ ] A test asserts `register` throws at registration time for an uncompilable schema, and\n      that the thrown message names the tool\n- [ ] A test asserts a function-valued tool definition whose returned schema differs between\n      calls is validated against the schema current at that call, not a stale cached one\n- [ ] A test asserts a static tool's schema is compiled once across ten `execute` calls\n- [ ] A tool-loop test in `chatOrchestrator.test.ts` drives a stub provider through a\n      rejected tool call followed by a corrected retry, and asserts `processMessage`\n      resolves — not rejects — with the rejection text visible to the provider on the next\n      round\n- [ ] `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name, unchanged,\n      pinned by a test\n- [ ] Rejection messages and log lines name the failing property paths and the constraint\n      that failed, and never a property **value**. Tool input is where identifying data\n      enters the engine and the engine redacts nothing today. A test asserts a rejected\n      payload containing an identifying field produces no log line and no returned text\n      containing that field's value\n- [ ] `execute` carries a **one-line** doc comment — `re-lint/max-comment-lines` rejects a\n      `@param`/`@returns`/`@throws` block — and the full contract (rejection `ToolResponse` for input\n      the schema rejects, throw only for an unknown tool name) is stated in\n      `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`, which the comment points at\n- [ ] The validator is in `package.json` `dependencies`, not `peerDependencies` or\n      `devDependencies`, and\n      `npm run build && node --input-type=module -e \"import('./dist/index.js')\"` exits `0`\n- [ ] `README.md` states what the registry now does, and the claim is true rather than\n      merely present\n- [ ] `docs/adding-a-tool.md` no longer tells executors to cast defensively and handle\n      missing input; it states that input reaching an executor has already passed the\n      declared schema, that unknown keys may still be present, and that a rejection is\n      returned to the model rather than thrown\n- [ ] `.specify/spec.md` records that a rejection is a tool result (not an exception), that\n      the tool-round bound is what stops a retry cycle from spinning, and that extra\n      properties pass through unmodified\n- [ ] The PR body states the migration note: an executor that used to hand-validate its\n      input now receives only input the schema already accepts, and a tool that relied on\n      seeing malformed input to produce its own error message stops seeing it. The version\n      bump is MINOR\n- [ ] Every test this issue adds cites the statement it validates with an inline\n      `([validated by ...])` link in a `specs/**` or `adrs/**` file, and the statements exist\n      before the tests do. `re-lint/require-spec-link` fails the build on an uncited test, so the\n      spec edits are part of this issue rather than a follow-up\n- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry for this change, written for somebody reading it on npm\n      rather than somebody reading this repo's log. CI runs `scripts/check-changelog.sh` and\n      fails any PR touching a non-test file under `src/` without one\n- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`)\n      and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the changed public surface\n\n## Tech notes\n\nToday's `throw` in `registry.ts` propagates through the orchestrator's tool-call\n`Promise.all`, out of the processing loop's `try`, fires `onError`, and reaches the\nWebSocket error handler, which sends a generic \"Failed to process message\" and never sends\nthe stream-end frame. That end-to-end path is why a rejection has to be a returned\n`ToolResponse` instead of a throw.\n\nDeliberately out of scope: the `Unknown tool: <name>` throw stays as-is — the same failure\nclass handled the opposite way, which is an intentional split, but changing it affects\ndirect callers and is tracked as *Return an unknown tool name to the model instead of\nthrowing*. Also out of scope: validating tool *output*; hallucination detection and an\nevaluation harness; per-tool authorization — a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still\nvalid, and authorization is a separate `ToolContext` boundary concern that no issue in this epic\nowns (*Let a consumer decline a tool call through a `beforeToolCall` hook* is a veto seam, not an\nauthorization model); retry, timeout\nand rate limiting around tool execution; and any actual tool implementation, which is\nwritten against this contract later.\n\n","numLines":126,"startLine":1,"totalLines":126}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_ad598d859b224ae58b3c13ca","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_636ce9cfe5b74878a121d4e8","type":"tool_result","content":"commit 74e2e570e9ab5573a1edf484769094798b414c1f\nAuthor: loop-bench <loop-bench@localhost>\nDate:   Mon Nov 2 23:57:13 2026 +0100\n\n    test(tools): pin input validation contract for issue 37\n    \n    Seven red acceptance tests against ToolRegistry.execute and register:\n    rejection ToolResponse before the executor runs, path-and-type error\n    messages without echoing values, register-time compile throw naming the\n    tool, per-call schema resolution for function sources, schema in force\n    across ten calls, the orchestrator retry loop, and value redaction in\n    results and logs. Two guards already green (pass-through of unlisted\n    properties, Unknown tool throw). Adds\n    specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md citing the tests and the\n    DoD triage.\n    \n    Relates to #37\n    \n    Co-Authored-By: Claude Code <noreply@anthropic.com>\n\n .lore/dod.md                                       |  78 +++++++\n specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md     |  40 ++++\n .../tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts              | 248 +++++++++++++++++++++\n 3 files changed, 366 insertions(+)\n----\ncommit 74e2e570e9ab5573a1edf484769094798b414c1f\nAuthor: loop-bench <loop-bench@localhost>\nDate:   Mon Nov 2 23:57:13 2026 +0100\n\n    test(tools): pin input validation contract for issue 37\n    \n    Seven red acceptance tests against ToolRegistry.execute and register:\n    rejection ToolResponse before the executor runs, path-and-type error\n    messages without echoing values, register-time compile throw naming the\n    tool, per-call schema resolution for function sources, schema in force\n    across ten calls, the orchestrator retry loop, and value redaction in\n    results and logs. Two guards already green (pass-through of unlisted\n    properties, Unknown tool throw). Adds\n    specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md citing the tests and the\n    DoD triage.\n    \n    Relates to #37\n    \n    Co-Authored-By: Claude Code <noreply@anthropic.com>\n\ndiff --git a/.lore/dod.md b/.lore/dod.md\nnew file mode 100644\nindex 0000000..8e06463\n--- /dev/null\n+++ b/.lore/dod.md\n@@ -0,0 +1,78 @@\n+# Definition of Done\n+\n+> \"`ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\n+> without ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\"\n+\n+**Strategy: `direct`** — the seam already exists: `ToolRegistry.register`/`execute` and\n+`createChatOrchestrator` are real public entry points callable today, so every test below\n+calls them directly and fails on the absent validation behaviour, not on a missing module.\n+`specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md` was created alongside the tests (the ticket\n+requires the statements to exist first, and `re-lint/require-spec-link` fails an uncited test);\n+the statements my tests validate cite them, the doc/packaging duties stay uncited until the\n+implementation lands.\n+\n+## Done when these pass\n+\n+- [ ] **rejects a call missing a required property before the executor runs** — `execute('get_weather', {})`\n+      returns a `ToolResponse` naming `location` as required and the executor's call count is 0 (ticket\n+      criteria 1-2: validate before the executor, rejection as returned `ToolResponse`)\n+      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n+- [ ] **names the property path and expected type when a field has the wrong type, without echoing the value** —\n+      a number where `location: string` is required is rejected with a message naming the path and the\n+      expected type and not containing the offending value, executor count 0 (criteria 1 and 4)\n+      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n+- [ ] **passes valid input with an unlisted extra property through to the executor unchanged, exactly once** —\n+      `toEqual` on what the executor receives, so an injected default or coerced value fails; GREEN TODAY\n+      BY DESIGN: this criterion pins pass-through behaviour the change must preserve, not behaviour absent\n+      today (criteria 3 and the \"no coercion/defaults\" clause)\n+      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n+- [ ] **compiles the schema at register time, throwing a message that names the tool** — `register` with\n+      an uncompilable schema throws naming `broken_tool` and registers nothing (criterion 5, \"fails at\n+      startup, not mid-conversation\") `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n+- [ ] **validates a function-valued definition against the schema current at each call** — a source whose\n+      returned schema starts requiring only `location` and then also `units` is honoured per call; the second\n+      call is rejected naming `units` and the executor ran once (criterion 6) — also the observable half of\n+      \"resolves and compiles per call\" `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n+- [ ] **holds the registered schema in force across ten execute calls** — ten calls alternating\n+      valid/invalid yield 5 rejections and 5 executor invocations (criterion 7's observable form: the schema\n+      governs every call; the raw compile count has no black-box observable without a validator-injection\n+      seam this ticket does not create — the register-time throw above pins the compile-once contract's\n+      other half) `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n+- [ ] **returns the rejection to the model so a corrected retry continues the conversation** — a stub\n+      provider drives tool_use with `{}`, and `processMessage` resolves with a `tool_result` naming\n+      `location` in the next round's messages and executor count 0 (criterion 8; written here rather than in\n+      `chatOrchestrator.test.ts` because acceptance tests live in their own `.acceptance.test.ts` file)\n+      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n+- [ ] **still throws Unknown tool for an unregistered name** — pins the unchanged throw; GREEN TODAY BY\n+      DESIGN (criterion 9) `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n+- [ ] **never echoes a rejected payload value into the result or the logs** — a rejected payload carrying\n+      an identifying value leaves it in neither the returned `result` nor any log line; log lines are\n+      observed through the logger's only sink (`console.log` in `src/shared/logger.ts`), captured and\n+      restored around the call — the seam, not a mock (criterion 10)\n+      `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n+\n+## Facets\n+\n+- Add the validator to `dependencies` (ajv unless the build proves otherwise) and compile a static\n+  tool's schema at `register`, throwing a message naming the tool for an uncompilable schema; for a\n+  function-valued source, resolve per call and cache the compiled validator by the resolved schema object\n+- Validate inside `execute` before the executor: rejection returns `{result: '<what was wrong>'}` naming\n+  the property path and constraint, no property values in the message or in any log line\n+- No orchestrator change should be needed for the loop test: `executeToolCalls` already feeds\n+  `response.result` back as the `tool_result`; if it fails, look at what `execute` returns, not at the loop\n+- One-line doc comment on `execute` pointing at `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`; full\n+  contract lives there (no `@param`/`@returns`/`@throws` block — `re-lint/max-comment-lines`)\n+- Docs the ticket owes: `README.md` claim made true, `docs/adding-a-tool.md` drops the cast-defensively\n+  guidance, `.specify/spec.md` records rejection-as-tool-result + round bound + extra properties pass\n+  through, `CHANGELOG.md` entry, MINOR version bump, PR body migration note\n+- The two green-by-design tests (pass-through, Unknown tool) must stay green unedited; the seven red\n+  tests go green with the implementation\n+\n+## Out of scope\n+\n+- Changing the `Unknown tool: <name>` throw into a returned tool result (own issue in this epic)\n+- Validating tool output, hallucination detection, an evaluation harness, per-tool {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_a1912cf7efe9444198005e72","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Tool Input Validation\n2\t\n3\t| Field  | Value                |\n4\t| ------ | -------------------- |\n5\t| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#37 |\n6\t| Status | In Progress          |\n7\t\n8\tEvery tool declares a JSON Schema `inputSchema` that is handed verbatim to the providers to shape the call the model produces. This spec makes the registry enforce that same schema where the model's call enters the engine: `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved definition's `inputSchema` before the executor runs, so a malformed call becomes a readable tool result the model can correct within the existing tool-round budget instead of an exception that ends the conversation. Validation enforces `required`, declared property types and `enum` only — it does not inject `additionalProperties: false`, coerce types, or apply defaults, and there is no opt-out flag.\n9\t\n10\t## The validation boundary\n11\t\n12\t- `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved tool definition's `inputSchema` before calling the executor ([validated by: rejects a call missing a required property before the executor runs](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L82)).\n13\t- A call the schema rejects returns a `ToolResponse` whose `result` describes the failure, rather than throwing ([validated by: rejects a call missing a required property before the executor runs](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L82)).\n14\t- The executor is never invoked for a rejected call ([validated by: names the property path and expected type when a field has the wrong type, without echoing the value](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L100)).\n15\t- A rejection names the failing property path and the constraint that failed, and never contains a property value ([validated by: names the property path and expected type when a field has the wrong type, without echoing the value](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L100)).\n16\t- No log line written for a rejected call contains a property value ([validated by: never echoes a rejected payload value into the result or the logs](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L232)).\n17\t- Input the schema accepts reaches the executor unchanged, exactly once per call: no injected defaults, no coercion, and unlisted extra properties pass through ([validated by: passes valid input with an unlisted extra property through to the executor unchanged, exactly once](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L115)).\n18\t\n19\t## Schema compilation\n20\t\n21\t- A static tool's schema is compiled once at `register` time, so an uncompilable schema throws at registration with a message naming the tool, and nothing is registered for it ([validated by: compiles the schema at register time, throwing a message that names the tool](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L128)).\n22\t- A static tool's schema stays in force across repeated `execute` calls ([validated by: holds the registered schema in force across ten execute calls](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L176)).\n23\t- A schema produced by a function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource` resolves and compiles per call, cached by the resolved schema object, so a definition whose schema differs between calls is validated against the schema current at that call, not a stale cached one ([validated by: validates a function-valued definition against the schema current at each call](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L147)).\n24\t\n25\t## Conversation flow\n26\t\n27\t- A rejected call is fed back to the model as a tool result, so the tool loop continues and a corrected retry can succeed within the same message ([validated by: returns the rejection to the model so a corrected retry continues the conversation](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L195)).\n28\t- The tool-round bound (`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5`) is what stops a rejection-and-retry cycle from spinning: each rejected call costs a round, so a model that never corrects its call is stopped by the existing round budget rather than by validation.\n29\t- `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name, unchanged ([validated by: still throws Unknown tool for an unregistered name](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L226)).\n30\t\n31\t## Out of scope\n32\t\n33\t- Validating tool _output_, hallucination detection, and an evaluation harness.\n34\t- Per-tool authorization: a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still valid, and authorization is a separate `ToolContext` boundary concern — _Let a consumer decline a tool call through a `beforeToolCall` hook_ is a veto seam, not an authorization model.\n35\t- Retry, timeout and rate limiting around tool execution, and any actual tool implementation, which is written against this contract later.\n36\t- Returning an unknown tool name to the model instead of throwing: the `Unknown tool: <name>` throw stays as-is and is tracked separately.\n37\t\n38\t## Packaging\n39\t\n40\t- The validator is a runtime dependency of the package, not a peer or dev dependency, and the compiled package still imports cleanly.\n41\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6a065b92-69cb-4751-8403-4aa4f3bdee06","uuid":"cd017f5a-2155-470d-91b6-0f2222fd76fd","timestamp":"2026-11-02T23:03:25.437Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/b3d496cb9011/workspace/target/specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md","content":"# Tool Input Validation\n\n| Field  | Value                |\n| ------ | -------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#37 |\n| Status | In Progress          |\n\nEvery tool declares a JSON Schema `inputSchema` that is handed verbatim to the providers to shape the call the model produces. This spec makes the registry enforce that same schema where the model's call enters the engine: `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved definition's `inputSchema` before the executor runs, so a malformed call becomes a readable tool result the model can correct within the existing tool-round budget instead of an exception that ends the conversation. Validation enforces `required`, declared property types and `enum` only — it does not inject `additionalProperties: false`, coerce types, or apply defaults, and there is no opt-out flag.\n\n## The validation boundary\n\n- `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved tool definition's `inputSchema` before calling the executor ([validated by: rejects a call missing a required property before the executor runs](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L82)).\n- A call the schema rejects returns a `ToolResponse` whose `result` describes the failure, rather than throwing ([validated by: rejects a call missing a required property before the executor runs](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L82)).\n- The executor is never invoked for a rejected call ([validated by: names the property path and expected type when a field has the wrong type, without echoing the value](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L100)).\n- A rejection names the failing property path and the constraint that failed, and never contains a property value ([validated by: names the property path and expected type when a field has the wrong type, without echoing the value](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L100)).\n- No log line written for a rejected call contains a property value ([validated by: never echoes a rejected payload value into the result or the logs](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L232)).\n- Input the schema accepts reaches the executor unchanged, exactly once per call: no injected defaults, no coercion, and unlisted extra properties pass through ([validated by: passes valid input with an unlisted extra property through to the executor unchanged, exactly once](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L115)).\n\n## Schema compilation\n\n- A static tool's schema is compiled once at `register` time, so an uncompilable schema throws at registration with a message naming the tool, and nothing is registered for it ([validated by: compiles the schema at register time, throwing a message that names the tool](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L128)).\n- A static tool's schema stays in force across repeated `execute` calls ([validated by: holds the registered schema in force across ten execute calls](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L176)).\n- A schema produced by a function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource` resolves and compiles per call, cached by the resolved schema object, so a definition whose schema differs between calls is validated against the schema current at that call, not a stale cached one ([validated by: validates a function-valued definition against the schema current at each call](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L147)).\n\n## Conversation flow\n\n- A rejected call is fed back to the model as a tool result, so the tool loop continues and a corrected retry can succeed within the same message ([validated by: returns the rejection to the model so a corrected retry continues the conversation](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L195)).\n- The tool-round bound (`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5`) is what stops a rejection-and-retry cycle from spinning: each rejected call costs a round, so a model that never corrects its call is stopped by the existing round budget rather than by validation.\n- `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name, unchanged ([validated by: still throws Unknown tool for an unregistered name](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L226)).\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- Validating tool _output_, hallucination detection, and an evaluation harness.\n- Per-tool authorization: a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still valid, and authorization is a separate `ToolContext` boundary concern — _Let a consumer decline a tool call through a `beforeToolCall` hook_ is a veto seam, not an authorization model.\n- Retry, timeout and rate limiting around tool execution, and any actual tool implementation, which is written against this contract later.\n- Returning an unknown tool name to the model instead of throwing: the `Unknown tool: <name>` throw stays as-is and is tracked separately.\n\n## Packaging\n\n- The validator is a runtime dependency of the package, not a peer or dev dependency, and the compiled package still imports cleanly.\n","numLines":41,"startLine":1,"totalLines":41}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_a1912cf7efe9444198005e72","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_44a01972bd1244dd97ea7968","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport {createChatOrchestrator} from '../chatOrchestrator.js';\n2\timport type {AIProvider, MessageChunk, SendMessageParams, ToolDefinition} from '../../types/ai.js';\n3\timport type {ChatSession} from '../../types/session.js';\n4\timport {PromptBuilder} from '../../infrastructure/builders/promptBuilder.js';\n5\timport {ToolRegistry} from './registry.js';\n6\timport type {ToolResponse} from './registry.js';\n7\t\n8\t// Fixed: nothing asserts on it, and new Date() would tie the run to the clock.\n9\tconst SENT_AT = '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z';\n10\t\n11\t// The schema under contract: location required, units declared, anything else unlisted.\n12\tconst weatherDefinition = (): ToolDefinition => ({\n13\t  name: 'get_weather',\n14\t  description: 'looks up the weather',\n15\t  inputSchema: {\n16\t    type: 'object',\n17\t    properties: {location: {type: 'string'}, units: {type: 'string'}},\n18\t    required: ['location'],\n19\t  },\n20\t});\n21\t\n22\t// A real executor that records what it was handed, so the tests can observe how often it ran.\n23\tconst recordingExecutor = () => {\n24\t  const seen: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];\n25\t  return {\n26\t    seen,\n27\t    run: async (input: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<string> => {\n28\t      seen.push(input);\n29\t      return '18C partly cloudy';\n30\t    },\n31\t  };\n32\t};\n33\t\n34\tfunction createSession(content: string): ChatSession {\n35\t  return {\n36\t    sessionId: 's1',\n37\t    userId: 'u1',\n38\t    entries: [{role: 'user', content, timestamp: SENT_AT}],\n39\t  };\n40\t}\n41\t\n42\t// The rejection as a value, with the logger's one sink captured around the call and restored either way.\n43\tconst executeWithLogCapture = async (\n44\t  registry: ToolRegistry,\n45\t  input: Record<string, unknown>\n46\t): Promise<{response: ToolResponse; lines: string[]}> => {\n47\t  const lines: string[] = [];\n48\t  const original = console.log;\n49\t  console.log = (...emitted: unknown[]) => {\n50\t    lines.push(emitted.map(String).join(' '));\n51\t  };\n52\t  try {\n53\t    return {response: await registry.execute('get_weather', input), lines};\n54\t  } finally {\n55\t    console.log = original;\n56\t  }\n57\t};\n58\t\n59\tconst captureThrow = (call: () => void): string => {\n60\t  try {\n61\t    call();\n62\t  } catch (error) {\n63\t    return error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);\n64\t  }\n65\t  return 'no throw';\n66\t};\n67\t\n68\t// One scripted round per sendMessage call, recording each request so a later round's view can be asserted.\n69\tconst scriptedProvider = (rounds: MessageChunk[][], requests: SendMessageParams[]): AIProvider => ({\n70\t  async *sendMessage(params: SendMessageParams): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n71\t    requests.push(params);\n72\t    for (const chunk of rounds[requests.length - 1] ?? []) yield chunk;\n73\t  },\n74\t  async generateStructured<T>(): Promise<T> {\n75\t    return {} as T;\n76\t  },\n77\t});\n78\t\n79\tconst promptBuilder = new PromptBuilder({identity: 'You are a test assistant.'});\n80\t\n81\tdescribe('ToolRegistry input validation', () => {\n82\t  it('rejects a call missing a required property before the executor runs', async () => {\n83\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n84\t    const tool = recordingExecutor();\n85\t    registry.register(weatherDefinition(), tool.run);\n86\t\n87\t    const response = await registry.execute('get_weather', {});\n88\t\n89\t    expect({\n90\t      response,\n91\t      namesLocationAsRequired: response.result.includes('location') && response.result.includes('required'),\n92\t      executorCalls: tool.seen.length,\n93\t    }).toEqual({\n94\t      response: {result: expect.any(String)},\n95\t      namesLocationAsRequired: true,\n96\t      executorCalls: 0,\n97\t    });\n98\t  });\n99\t\n100\t  it('names the property path and expected type when a field has the wrong type, without echoing the value', async () => {\n101\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n102\t    const tool = recordingExecutor();\n103\t    registry.register(weatherDefinition(), tool.run);\n104\t\n105\t    const response = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 123456789});\n106\t\n107\t    expect({\n108\t      namesPath: response.result.includes('location'),\n109\t      namesExpectedType: response.result.includes('string'),\n110\t      echoesValue: response.result.includes('123456789'),\n111\t      executorCalls: tool.seen.length,\n112\t    }).toEqual({namesPath: true, namesExpectedType: true, echoesValue: false, executorCalls: 0});\n113\t  });\n114\t\n115\t  it('passes valid input with an unlisted extra property through to the executor unchanged, exactly once', async () => {\n116\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n117\t    const tool = recordingExecutor();\n118\t    registry.register(weatherDefinition(), tool.run);\n119\t\n120\t    const response = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 'Berlin', units: 'celsius', extra: 'kept'});\n121\t\n122\t    expect({response, handedToExecutor: tool.seen}).toEqual({\n123\t      response: {result: '18C partly cloudy'},\n124\t      handedToExecutor: [{location: 'Berlin', units: 'celsius', extra: 'kept'}],\n125\t    });\n126\t  });\n127\t\n128\t  it('compiles the schema at register time, throwing a message that names the tool', () => {\n129\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n130\t    const thrown = captureThrow(() =>\n131\t      registry.register(\n132\t        {\n133\t          ...weatherDefinition(),\n134\t          name: 'broken_tool',\n135\t          inputSchema: {type: 'object', properties: {location: {type: 'strng'}}, required: ['location']},\n136\t        },\n137\t        async () => '18C partly cloudy'\n138\t      )\n139\t    );\n140\t\n141\t    expect({registered: registry.has('broken_tool'), thrown}).toEqual({\n142\t      registered: false,\n143\t      thrown: expect.stringContaining('broken_tool'),\n144\t    });\n145\t  });\n146\t\n147\t  it('validates a function-valued definition against the schema current at each call', async () => {\n148\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n149\t    const tool = recordingExecutor();\n150\t    let requireUnits = false;\n151\t    registry.register(\n152\t      () => ({\n153\t        ...weatherDefinition(),\n154\t        inputSchema: requireUnits\n155\t          ? {\n156\t              type: weatherDefinition().inputSchema,\n161\t      }),\n162\t      tool.run\n163\t    );\n164\t\n165\t    const before = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 'Berlin'});\n166\t    requireUnits = true;\n167\t    const after = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 'Berlin'});\n168\t\n169\t    expect({before, after, executorCalls: tool.seen.length}).toEqual({\n170\t      before: {result: '18C partly cloudy'},\n171\t      after: {result: expect.stringContaining('units')},\n172\t      executorCalls: 1,\n173\t    });\n174\t  });\n175\t\n176\t  it('holds the registered schema in force across ten execute calls', async () => {\n177\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n178\t    const tool = recordingExecutor();\n179\t    registry.register(weatherDefinition(), tool.run);\n180\t\n181\t    const responses: ToolResponse[] = [];\n182\t    for (let round = 0; round < 10; round++) {\n183\t      responses.push(\n184\t        await registry.execute('get_weather', round % 2 === 0 ? {location: 'Berlin'} : {units: 'celsius'})\n185\t      );\n186\t    }\n187\t\n188\t    expect({\n189\t      accepted: responses.filter(r => r.result === '18C partly cloudy').length,\n190\t      rejected: responses.filter(r => r.result !== '18C partly cloudy').length,\n191\t      executorCalls: tool.seen.length,\n192\t    }).toEqual({accepted: 5, rejected: 5, executorCalls: 5});\n193\t  });\n194\t\n195\t  it('returns the rejection to the model so a corrected retry continues the conversation', async () => {\n196\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n197\t    const tool = recordingExecutor();\n198\t    registry.register(weatherDefinition(), tool.run);\n199\t    const requests: SendMessageParams[] = [];\n200\t    const provider = scriptedProvider(\n201\t      [\n202\t        [\n203\t          {type: 'tool_use', toolCall: {id: 'call-1', name: 'get_weather', input: {}}},\n204\t          {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_use'},\n205\t        ],\n206\t        [\n207\t          {type: 'text', text: 'recovered'},\n208\t          {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn'},\n209\t        ],\n210\t      ],\n211\t      requests\n212\t    );\n213\t    const orchestrator = createChatOrchestrator(provider, promptBuilder, registry);\n214\t\n215\t    const text = await orchestrator.processMessage(createSession('weather in Berlin?'));\n216\t    const secondRound = requests[1]?.messages ?? [];\n217\t    const toolResults = secondRound.flatMap(m => (m.role === 'user' && Array.isArray(m.content) ? m.content : []));\n218\t\n219\t    expect({text, toolResults, executorCalls: tool.seen.length}).toEqual({\n220\t      text: 'recovered',\n221\t      toolResults: [{type: 'tool_result', toolUseId: 'call-1', content: expect.stringContaining('location')}],\n222\t      executorCalls: 0,\n223\t    });\n224\t  });\n225\t\n226\t  it('still throws Unknown tool for an unregistered name', async () => {\n227\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n228\t\n229\t    await expect(registry.execute('no_such_tool', {})).rejects.toThrow('Unknown tool: no_such_tool');\n230\t  });\n231\t\n232\t  it('never echoes a rejected payload value into the result or the logs', async () => {\n233\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n234\t    const tool = recordingExecutor();\n235\t    registry.register(weatherDefinition(), tool.run);\n236\t\n237\t    const {response, lines} = await executeWithLogCapture(registry, {location: '42 Sub Rings,Inner City', units: 5});\n238\t    const seen = `${response.result}\\n${lines.join('\\n')}`;\n239\t\n240\t    expect({\n241\t      rejection: response.result,\n242\t      identifyingValueLeaked: seen.includes('42 Sub Rings,Inner City'),\n243\t    }).toEqual({\n244\t      rejection: expect.stringContaining('units'),\n245\t      identifyingValueLeaked: false,\n246\t    });\n247\t  });\n248\t});\n249\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6a065b92-69cb-4751-8403-4aa4f3bdee06","uuid":"e6352eaa-e172-43de-aca4-c81fe49fe416","timestamp":"2026-11-02T23:03:25.799Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/b3d496cb9011/workspace/target/src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts","content":"import {createChatOrchestrator} from '../chatOrchestrator.js';\nimport type {AIProvider, MessageChunk, SendMessageParams, ToolDefinition} from '../../types/ai.js';\nimport type {ChatSession} from '../../types/session.js';\nimport {PromptBuilder} from '../../infrastructure/builders/promptBuilder.js';\nimport {ToolRegistry} from './registry.js';\nimport type {ToolResponse} from './registry.js';\n\n// Fixed: nothing asserts on it, and new Date() would tie the run to the clock.\nconst SENT_AT = '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z';\n\n// The schema under contract: location required, units declared, anything else unlisted.\nconst weatherDefinition = (): ToolDefinition => ({\n  name: 'get_weather',\n  description: 'looks up the weather',\n  inputSchema: {\n    type: 'object',\n    properties: {location: {type: 'string'}, units: {type: 'string'}},\n    required: ['location'],\n  },\n});\n\n// A real executor that records what it was handed, so the tests can observe how often it ran.\nconst recordingExecutor = () => {\n  const seen: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];\n  return {\n    seen,\n    run: async (input: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<string> => {\n      seen.push(input);\n      return '18C partly cloudy';\n    },\n  };\n};\n\nfunction createSession(content: string): ChatSession {\n  return {\n    sessionId: 's1',\n    userId: 'u1',\n    entries: [{role: 'user', content, timestamp: SENT_AT}],\n  };\n}\n\n// The rejection as a value, with the logger's one sink captured around the call and restored either way.\nconst executeWithLogCapture = async (\n  registry: ToolRegistry,\n  input: Record<string, unknown>\n): Promise<{response: ToolResponse; lines: string[]}> => {\n  const lines: string[] = [];\n  const original = console.log;\n  console.log = (...emitted: unknown[]) => {\n    lines.push(emitted.map(String).join(' '));\n  };\n  try {\n    return {response: await registry.execute('get_weather', input), lines};\n  } finally {\n    console.log = original;\n  }\n};\n\nconst captureThrow = (call: () => void): string => {\n  try {\n    call();\n  } catch (error) {\n    return error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);\n  }\n  return 'no throw';\n};\n\n// One scripted round per sendMessage call, recording each request so a later round's view can be asserted.\nconst scriptedProvider = (rounds: MessageChunk[][], requests: SendMessageParams[]): AIProvider => ({\n  async *sendMessage(params: SendMessageParams): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n    requests.push(params);\n    for (const chunk of rounds[requests.length - 1] ?? []) yield chunk;\n  },\n  async generateStructured<T>(): Promise<T> {\n    return {} as T;\n  },\n});\n\nconst promptBuilder = new PromptBuilder({identity: 'You are a test assistant.'});\n\ndescribe('ToolRegistry input validation', () => {\n  it('rejects a call missing a required property before the executor runs', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    const tool = recordingExecutor();\n    registry.register(weatherDefinition(), tool.run);\n\n    const response = await registry.execute('get_weather', {});\n\n    expect({\n      response,\n      namesLocationAsRequired: response.result.includes('location') && response.result.includes('required'),\n      executorCalls: tool.seen.length,\n    }).toEqual({\n      response: {result: expect.any(String)},\n      namesLocationAsRequired: true,\n      executorCalls: 0,\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('names the property path and expected type when a field has the wrong type, without echoing the value', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    const tool = recordingExecutor();\n    registry.register(weatherDefinition(), tool.run);\n\n    const response = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 123456789});\n\n    expect({\n      namesPath: response.result.includes('location'),\n      namesExpectedType: response.result.includes('string'),\n      echoesValue: response.result.includes('123456789'),\n      executorCalls: tool.seen.length,\n    }).toEqual({namesPath: true, namesExpectedType: true, echoesValue: false, executorCalls: 0});\n  });\n\n  it('passes valid input with an unlisted extra property through to the executor unchanged, exactly once', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    const tool = recordingExecutor();\n    registry.register(weatherDefinition(), tool.run);\n\n    const response = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 'Berlin', units: 'celsius', extra: 'kept'});\n\n    expect({response, handedToExecutor: tool.seen}).toEqual({\n      response: {result: '18C partly cloudy'},\n      handedToExecutor: [{location: 'Berlin', units: 'celsius', extra: 'kept'}],\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('compiles the schema at register time, throwing a message that names the tool', () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    const thrown = captureThrow(() =>\n      registry.register(\n        {\n          ...weatherDefinition(),\n          name: 'broken_tool',\n          inputSchema: {type: 'object', properties: {location: {type: 'strng'}}, required: ['location']},\n        },\n        async () => '18C partly cloudy'\n      )\n    );\n\n    expect({registered: registry.has('broken_tool'), thrown}).toEqual({\n      registered: false,\n      thrown: expect.stringContaining('broken_tool'),\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('validates a function-valued definition against the schema current at each call', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    const tool = recordingExecutor();\n    let requireUnits = false;\n    registry.register(\n      () => ({\n        ...weatherDefinition(),\n        inputSchema: requireUnits\n          ? {\n              type: 'object',\n              properties: {location: {type: 'string'}, units: {type: 'string'}},\n              required: ['location', 'units'],\n            }\n          : weatherDefinition().inputSchema,\n      }),\n      tool.run\n    );\n\n    const before = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 'Berlin'});\n    requireUnits = true;\n    const after = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 'Berlin'});\n\n    expect({before, after, executorCalls: tool.seen.length}).toEqual({\n      before: {result: '18C partly cloudy'},\n      after: {result: expect.stringContaining('units')},\n      executorCalls: 1,\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('holds the registered schema in force across ten execute calls', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    const tool = recordingExecutor();\n    registry.register(weatherDefinition(), tool.run);\n\n    const responses: ToolResponse[] = [];\n    for (let round = 0; round < 10; round++) {\n      responses.push(\n        await registry.execute('get_weather', round % 2 === 0 ? {location: 'Berlin'} : {units: 'celsius'})\n      );\n    }\n\n    expect({\n      accepted: responses.filter(r => r.result === '18C partly cloudy').length,\n      rejected: responses.filter(r => r.result !== '18C partly cloudy').length,\n      executorCalls: tool.seen.length,\n    }).toEqual({accepted: 5, rejected: 5, executorCalls: 5});\n  });\n\n  it('returns the rejection to the model so a corrected retry continues the conversation', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    const tool = recordingExecutor();\n    registry.register(weatherDefinition(), tool.run);\n    const requests: SendMessageParams[] = [];\n    const provider = scriptedProvider(\n      [\n        [\n          {type: 'tool_use', toolCall: {id: 'call-1', name: 'get_weather', input: {}}},\n          {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_use'},\n        ],\n        [\n          {type: 'text', text: 'recovered'},\n          {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn'},\n        ],\n      ],\n      requests\n    );\n    const orchestrator = createChatOrchestrator(provider, promptBuilder, registry);\n\n    const text = await orchestrator.processMessage(createSession('weather in Berlin?'));\n    const secondRound = requests[1]?.messages ?? [];\n    const toolResults = secondRound.flatMap(m => (m.role === 'user' && Array.isArray(m.content) ? m.content : []));\n\n    expect({text, toolResults, executorCalls: tool.seen.length}).toEqual({\n      text: 'recovered',\n      toolResults: [{type: 'tool_result', toolUseId: 'call-1', content: expect.stringContaining('location')}],\n      executorCalls: 0,\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('still throws Unknown tool for an unregistered name', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n\n    await expect(registry.execute('no_such_tool', {})).rejects.toThrow('Unknown tool: no_such_tool');\n  });\n\n  it('never echoes a rejected payload value into the result or the logs', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    const tool = recordingExecutor();\n    registry.register(weatherDefinition(), tool.run);\n\n    const {response, lines} = await executeWithLogCapture(registry, {location: '42 Sub Rings,Inner City', units: 5});\n    const seen = `${response.result}\\n${lines.join('\\n')}`;\n\n    expect({\n      rejection: response.result,\n      identifyingValueLeaked: seen.includes('42 Sub Rings,Inner City'),\n    }).toEqual({\n      rejection: expect.stringContaining('units'),\n      identifyingValueLeaked: false,\n    });\n  });\n});\n","numLines":249,"startLine":1,"totalLines":249}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_44a01972bd1244dd97ea7968","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

Jest has no dry-run, so the list command executes the suite\n    once with --json and derives every name from the report; it deliberately\n    tolerates a red suite, since listing must not depend on the tests passing.\n    The run command selects one test with --runTestsByPath plus an anchored,\n    regex-escaped -t, and treats a selector that matches nothing as a failure\n    rather than a green run of an empty suite. Neither command builds first:\n    no test here reads dist/.\n  list: node scripts/lore-list-tests.mjs\n  run: node scripts/lore-run-test.mjs \"{selector}\"\n  coverage_format: lcov\n\n----\nnode_modules/.bin/jest","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6a065b92-69cb-4751-8403-4aa4f3bdee06","uuid":"1ece3ad8-8e88-4fea-9a71-b61e6d9cba7a","timestamp":"2026-11-02T23:04:05.848Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"check-action-pins.mjs\ncheck-action-pins.test.ts\ncheck-audit.mjs\ncheck-audit.test.ts\ncheck-changelog.sh\ncheck-changelog.test.ts\ncheck-doc-blocks.mjs\ncheck-doc-blocks.test.ts\ncheck-doc-gates.test.ts\ncheck-doc-paths.mjs\ncheck-doc-regions.mjs\ncheck-spec-anchor-names.mjs\ncheck-spec-anchor-names.test.ts\ncheck-spec-links.mjs\ncheck-spec-links.test.ts\ncheck-spec-status.mjs\ncheck-spec-status.test.ts\ncheck-spike-status.mjs\ncheck-version.sh\ncheck-version.test.ts\neslint-layers.test.ts\neslint-spec-docs.test.ts\nfixtures\nhelpers\nlib\nlore-list-tests.mjs\nlore-run-test.mjs\nrepoint-spec-anchors.mjs\nrepoint-spec-anchors.test.ts\nsmoke.sh\nverify-script.test.ts\n\n----\n# Lore test-command interface (lore_list_tests / lore_run_test).\n# Written for this repo's Jest suite rather than adapted from the Vitest\n# equivalent for a Vitest suite: the report shapes happen to match, but the\n# invocations do not.\n- id: hal-engine-jest\n  name: HAL Engine Jest suite\n  cwd: .\n  description: >-\n    The ts-jest unit suite over src/ and scripts/ (jest.config.js roots), run\n    on every pull request in .github/workflows/ci.yml as the \"Test\" step.\n    Selectors are \"<repo-relative-file>::<full test name>\" as emitted by the\n    list command. Jest has no dry-run, so the list command executes the suite\n    once with --json and derives every name from the report; it deliberately\n    tolerates a red suite, since listing must not depend on the tests passing.\n    The run command selects one test with --runTestsByPath plus an anchored,\n    regex-escaped -t, and treats a selector that matches nothing as a failure\n    rather than a green run of an empty suite.
